`ctx = { bpm = 120, smooth = 1, fx = null, qr = null, ultra = 0, mpeBendX = 0, mpeYBi = 0 }`: `fx` = the track's FX state (needed to resolve dynamic FX destinations; absent ⇒ FX rows inactive), `qr` = quality-reduction caps (§14.3), `ultra` = render at Ultra (pref, bounces only), `mpeBendX`/`mpeYBi` = the two MPE app preferences (§13.10; passed like `smooth`; a preference change recompiles every Serum track, bounces use the current preferences). Pure, deterministic, never throws for normalized input; ≤ 2 ms typical, ≤ 4 ms dense (64 rows with curves, 10 LFOs at 480 points). Host-facing fields as Phase-1 §4.1 plus:

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| `needs` | every asset/table id the patch can play (Phase-1 rule "possibly on" extended to OSC C, sample types, colour noise needs nothing, Convolve IRs come from `compileFx` and are merged by the host): tables `osc[i].wt` (type wt), sub tables (`SUB_IDS` in S1 mode, `SUB_IDS2` otherwise; all of them if the shape destination is targeted), `noise.src` (sample noises), `osc[i].smp` playback entry id (sample/gran; a wavetable id gives `wtsmp:<table id>`, §9.4.8; plus `~<opsKey>` when `trim`/`fade` are set), its spectral analysis id `spec:<entry id>` (spec; `~r` appended when `ops.rev`), `osc[i].multi` and every zone sample of it (resolved by the host) |
| `serum` | CP v2 (below) |
| `serumT` | CT v2 (below) as **parts**; the host sends only the parts whose key changed since the node last received them (§4.1 CT) |
| `seq`, `seqT` | SQP and its tables (§4.4), part-keyed like `serumT` |
| `mode`, `poly`, `legato`, `prio` | as Phase 1 |

**CP v2** — every field always present, fixed shapes:

| Field | Type | Length | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| `ver` | number | — | 2 |
| `NSV`, `NSRC`, `NROW` | number | — | 429, 62, 64 |
| `use` | number | — | feature bits for cheap bypass: 1 OSC C possibly on · 2 Filter 2 possibly on · 4 any warp slot 2 ≠ Off (base or targeted) · 8 a Sample/Multisample osc · 16 Granular · 32 Spectral · 64 any bus send > 0 or bus route · 128 any Direct route · 256 MPE on · 512 tuning loaded · 1024 Voice Control active · 2048 LFO 9/10 or ENV 4 used · 4096 any row with `crv`, `rise`, `fall` or `acurve` · 8192 sequencer active (§7.1) · 16384 S1 mode |
| `base` | Float64Array | 429 | eff base per vi (virtual destinations per §3.11.3; slots 403–405 of the scope-M rows stay 0) |
| `mN` | number | — | active rows compacted in slot order |
| `mSrc`, `mAux` | Int8Array | 64 | source indices |
| `mDst` | Int16Array | 64 | ≥ 0: vi; < 0: FX-ctx index `−1 − k` |
| `mAmt`, `mK`, `mAK` | Float64Array | 64 | `amt·out`; `MC_K·curve`; `MC_K·acurve` |
| `mBi`, `mInv`, `mCtx`, `mOn` | Uint8Array | 64 | Phase-1 semantics |
| `mCrv` | Int8Array | 64 | slot of the editable curve in `CT.crv` or −1 |
| `mRise`, `mFall` | Float64Array | 64 | seconds (0 = no slew) |
| `mRd` | Uint8Array | 64 | 1 if the row reads `randd` (src or aux) |
| `mAlias` | Uint8Array | 64 | 1 if the row targets the other mode's field of an S1 pair and was re-scaled onto the active one (§2.6.4) |
| `srcUsed`, `srcBi` | Uint8Array | 62 | used sources; native polarity (Phase-1 `SRC_POL`, `mpey` bipolar when `ctx.mpeYBi`) |
| `modded` | Uint8Array | 429 | voice-context targets |
| `oType`, `phMem`, `phLeg`, `smoothWT`, `pMode`, `bendTrack`, `uniMode`, `spawn` | Uint8Array | 3 | per oscillator |
| `pSrc` | Int8Array | 3 | ratio source: −1 note, 0–2 osc |
| `uniRange` | Float64Array | 3 | |
| `fmType` | Uint8Array | 6 | `[o·2 + slot]` |
| `wt`, `smp`, `spec`, `multi` | Array(3) of string | 3 | asset ids ('' = none) |
| `memSet` | Float64Array | 48 | `[o·16 + i]` |
| `mapLo`, `mapHi`, `mapVLo`, `mapVHi`, `mapFold`, `mapWarp` | Uint8Array | 6 | channels A, B, C, Noise, Sub, ARP |
| sample options `sLoop`, `sRel`, `sLink`, `sExit`, `sLoopGr`, `sScanKey`, `sScanLock`, `sLenOn`, `sSlMode` (0 when `xyOn`), `sSlEnd`, `sSlOne`, `sSlRoot`, `sWin`, `sDens`, `sDensJump`, `sGlen`, `sPhLock`, `sTrans`, `sSmooth`, `sPostWarp`, `sNorm`, `sRev`, `xyOn`, `xyY`, `xyMode`, `msOver`, `msDlyBpm` | Uint8Array | 3 | §2.2.3, §2.2.4; `sNorm`/`sRev` = `smpo.ops.norm`/`.rev` applied by the DSP (gain `1/peak` of the entry; reversed reads, §6.7.7) |
| `sScanR` | Float64Array | 3 | 2/4/8 |
| `lenLad` | Float64Array | 15 | `LEN_LADDER` beats (eff `k.lenrate` index → beats, §6.7.2) |
| `sMaxGr`, `sSlN` | Uint16Array | 3 | max grains; slice count |
| `sSlPos` | Float64Array | 3·258 | slice boundaries per osc: `[0, marks…, 1]` zero-padded |
| `sDensLad`, `sGlenLad` | Float64Array | 3·27 | `gridLadder(dot, trip)` beats per osc (zero-padded) |
| `sDensLadN`, `sGlenLadN` | Uint8Array | 3 | ladder lengths |
| `route`, `envOn` | Uint8Array | 7 | channels A, B, C, Noise, Sub, F1, F2 |
| `fKey`, `fClean` | Uint8Array | 2 | |
| `pz` | Float64Array | 24 | PZ SVF corners `[k·12 + corner·3 + {lp, bp, hp}]` |
| `subContig`, `noiseOneshot`, `noiseKey` | number | — | |
| `noiseId` | string | — | sample noise id ('' for colour noise) |
| `noiseColor` | number | — | −1 sample noise, 0–3 colour noise |
| `envBpm`, `envLegInv`, `envStealRe` | Uint8Array | 4 | |
| `envLad` | Float64Array | 16 | `ENV_LADDER` beats |
| `lfoMode`, `lfoMono`, `lfoType`, `lfoDir`, `lfoBpm`, `lfoAnchor`, `lfoX10`, `lfoSwing`, `lfoLadN` | Uint8Array | 10 | |
| `lfoLad` | Float64Array | 10·43 | Phase-1 layout, 10 LFOs |
| `chaosMono`, `chaosSh` | Uint8Array | 2 | Phase 1 |
| `macro` | Float64Array | 8 | |
| `mpeMac` | Int8Array | 8 | −1, or 0 X / 1 Y / 2 Z driving that macro (§6.17) |
| `mono`, `legato`, `poly`, `always`, `scaled`, `oneNote`, `steal`, `veloPorta`, `notePorta` | number | — | |
| `vcLen`, `vcLMode` | number | — | 1–8; `vc.lmode` |
| `vcRates` | Float64Array | 11 | `VC_RATES` |
| `vcSteps` | Float64Array | 48 | `[j·6 + {pan, det, cut, env, m1, m2}]` |
| `vcSel` | Uint8Array | 5 | A, B, C, N, S |
| `os`, `a4`, `s1`, `smooth` | number | — | `smooth` = 0.01 when `global.nosmooth`, else `ctx.smooth` |
| `tune` | Float64Array | 128 | key → fractional pitch (identity `n` when no tuning) |
| `sclN`, `sclPer` | number | — | Step mode: degrees per period, period in semitones (0 when no `.scl`) |
| `sclSteps` | Float64Array | 128 | degree k → semitones (k < `sclN`) |
| `mpeOn`, `mpeBend`, `mpeBendX`, `mpeWheelY` | number | — | §6.17 (`mpeBendX` = `ctx.mpeBendX`) |
| `outMask` | number | — | voice output pairs in use: 1 main · 2 direct · 4 bus1 · 8 bus2 |
| `fxKey`, `NFX` | string, number | — | FX layout key and FX-ctx length (§4.2) the matrix was compiled against |
| `qr` | Float64Array | 5 | `[uniMax 1–16, grainsMax 16–256, specHopMul 1|2|4, specStreams 1–64, grainsProc 64–1024]` (defaults `[16, 256, 1, 32, 512]`; touch devices `[16, 256, 1, 16, 256]`, §14.2) |
| `ctKey` | string | — | key of the whole CT (concatenation of the part keys) |

**CT v2** (`patch.serumT`), memoised by content: Phase-1 CT fields extended to 10 LFOs and 3 remaps, plus the fields below. It is delivered **by part** (B13: a whole CT is ≈ 190 KB and an LFO/path drag would re-send it every animation frame): `serumT = {key, parts: {<name>: {key, …fields}}}` with the parts `lfo0…lfo9` (that LFO's `lfoTab` slice, points, point links, start, loop), `path0…path9`, `remap0…remap2`, `crv0…crv63` (one row curve each), `spec0…spec2`, `velo`, `note`. The host keeps, per node, the last part keys it posted and sends only changed parts (`sendPatch`); the DSP merges received parts into its preallocated CT arrays (`SerumDSP.mergeCT`, allocation-free) and adopts `ctKey`. Integration test: one frame of an LFO drag (≤ 64 points), a path drag, a matrix-curve drag and a clip-note drag each post ≤ 12 KB.

| Field | Type | Length | Content |
|---|---|---|---|
| `key` | string | — | FNV-1a over every graph (10 LFO graphs + start/loop, 10 paths, 3 remaps, velo, note, 64 row curves, 3 spectral filter curves) in that order (= the concatenated part keys) |
| `lfoTab` | Float32Array | 10·1025 | |
| `lfoPtsOff` | Uint16Array | 11 | |
| `lfoPts`, `lfoPm`, `lfoPmAny`, `lfoStart`, `lfoLoop` | as Phase 1 | 10 LFOs | `lfoStart[l]` = `pts[start].x` exactly as Phase 1 (may be 1 = the last point; no `frac`, §6.15.2) |
| `pathX`, `pathY` | Float32Array | 10·1025 | Path LFOs resampled by arc length (§6.15.3); zeros otherwise |
| `remap` | Float32Array | 3·2049 | |
| `veloTab`, `noteTab` | Float32Array | 129 | |
| `crv` | Float32Array | 64·129 | `[slot·129 + j]` = row curve at j/128 (identity for rows without one) |
| `specFilt` | Float32Array | 3·513 | spectral filter gain at x = j/512 (x = log-frequency 20 Hz…20 kHz) |

### 4.2 FX layout and the FX context (owner F1)

`fxLayout(fx) → { key, inst: [{id, type, v, rack, parent, branch, off, n}], NP }` (pure, memoised): instances in processing order — rack 0, rack 1, rack 2; inside a rack the list order, a splitter followed depth-first by branch 0, 1, 2 lists. `off` = cumulative parameter offset, `n` = the table length. `key` = FNV-1a of `id|type|v|rack|parent|branch` for every instance. `NFX = 8 + NP` (≤ 8 + 48·24).

The FX-context vector (Float64Array(NFX)) is the shared language between `SerumDSP.fxMods` (offsets) and `FXRack` (bases + offsets): `[0]` g.master · `[1]` g.bypass · `[2]` g.direct · `[3]` g.bus1v · `[4]` g.bus2v · `[5…7]` 0 · `[8 + off + p]` parameter p of instance i.

### 4.3 `compileFx(fx, ctx) → FXP v2` (`patch.fxr`, every model) and FXT v2

`ctx = { bpm = 120, rate = 48000, master, bypass, direct, bus1v, bus2v, modded = null (Uint8Array(NFX)), seed = false, smooth = 1, silence = 1, qr = null }` (Serum tracks pass the five gains from `s.p`; others nothing ⇒ master 1 (n 1 with the pow3 law), bypass 0, direct/bus n 0.5 = 0 dB; `rate` = the AudioContext rate the patch is for — live context rate, or the bounce rate — used to name the shaped IR assets). `modded` comes from `fxModded2(s, fx)` (§11.1).

| Field | Type | Content |
|---|---|---|
| `ver`, `NFX`, `NP`, `key` | | 2, sizes, layout key |
| `base` | Float64Array(NFX) | `[0…4]` from ctx; params from `inst.p` |
| `nI` | number | instance count |
| `iType`, `iVar`, `iRack`, `iBr`, `iN`, `iByp`, `iAlloc` | Uint8Array(nI) | `iAlloc` = enable base ≥ 0.5 or `modded[enable]` (Phase-1 lazy allocation rule per instance) |
| `iPar` | Int16Array(nI) | splitter instance index or −1 |
| `iOff` | Int32Array(nI) | parameter offsets (into `base` minus 8) |
| `iBB` | Uint8Array(3·nI) | branch bypasses (splitters) |
| `iPh` | Float64Array(nI) | saved phases |
| `iId` | Array(nI) of string | instance ids (the DSP keeps module state across edits by id, §8.1) |
| `iRev`, `iCho` | Uint8Array(nI) | S1 reverb type / chorus mono flag |
| `iIr` | Array(nI) of string | Convolve shaped-IR entry id `<ir id>@<rate>~<shapeKey>` (`shapeKey` = 8-hex FNV-1a of the six `nomod` base values quantised to 1/256, §8.4), '' for other types |
| `rByp`, `rRoute` | Uint8Array(3) | rack bypass, bus routes (rack 1, 2) |
| `needs` | Array of string | shaped-IR entry ids in use (merged into the patch `needs` by the host; the asset manager builds them with `smpwork` op `irshape`, §9.4.1) |
| `filTypeAll` | Uint8Array(nI) | per FX filter instance: type targeted ⇒ preallocate all families |
| `seed`, `any`, `bpm`, `smooth`, `silence` | number | Phase-1 semantics |
| `convMax` | number | Convolve tail cap in seconds (`qr`, §14.3; default 10) |
| `xsKey` | string | key of the FXT |

**FXT v2** (`patch.fxrT`) = `{ key, xs: [{id, xsA: Float32Array(1025), xsB: Float32Array(1025)}] }` for every Distortion instance (any variant), memoised by content; delivered and stripped like Phase 1.

### 4.4 Sequencer patch (`patch.seq`, `patch.seqT`; owner D6, `compileSeq(s, ctx)` in `seq-core.js`, called by `compileSerum`)

| Field | Content |
|---|---|
| `seq.ver` | 1 |
| `seq.key` | key of the `seqT` it was compiled with |
| `seq.active` | 1 if `arp.on` or `clip.on` is possibly on, or `kbd.trans ≠ 0`, or `kbd.key ≥ 0`, or a trigger octave is set with its module possibly on, or any osc/arp map is not full range |
| `seq.kbd` | Float64Array(8): trans, key, scale mask (bit i = semitone i in scale), swing s ∈ [−1, 1], swing division (beats), arp map lo, hi (key), arp fold |
| `seq.arpG`, `seq.clipG` | Float64Array(8): quant beats, midiOut, trigOct, sel, (clip) trigger mode, preview index, outTrack slot (index into `seq.outIds`, −1 none) |
| `seq.outIds` | Array of string: MIDI OUT target track ids (arp, clip; §7.9) |
| `seqT.key` | FNV-1a over the arp and clip state (= the concatenated part keys) |
| `seqT.parts` | delivery by part (B13), like CT: `arp0…arp11` (that slot's row of `seqT.arp`, its events, strums and lanes) and `clip0…clip11` (that clip's row, notes and lanes); the host posts only changed parts and `SxSeq.Seq.merge(part)` copies them into the preallocated arrays below (offsets are recomputed by the sequencer, not trusted from the part) |
| `seqT.arp` | Float64Array(12·40): per slot `[shape, bpm, trip, dot, n.rate, n.hz, n.shift, n.range, n.offset, n.repeats, n.gate, n.chance, n.rrate, n.vdecay, n.vtarget, n.trans, n.wtrans, n.wrange, rshape, latch, thru, chancePre, rLaunch, rRate, rNote, rFirst, vel, vRetrig, pat.len, pat.mode, time beats, pat.stepMode, pat.wrap, evOff, evN, strumOff, strumN, laneMask, laneOff, 0]` |
| `seqT.arpEv` | Float64Array(5·Σev): `[t16, len16, row, vel, accent]` sorted by t16 |
| `seqT.arpStrum` | Float64Array(2·Σstrum) |
| `seqT.arpLane` | Float32Array(129·Σlanes): lanes vel, gate, chance, x in that order per slot (mask bits 1, 2, 4, 8) |
| `seqT.clip` | Float64Array(12·24): per clip `[len, start, off, offQ beats, kb, mode, time beats, quant beats, retrig, veloTrig, noteGate, n.trans, n.rate, n.offset, notesOff, notesN, laneMask (8 bits m1–m8), laneOff, 0…]` |
| `seqT.clipNotes` | Float64Array(8·Σnotes): `[t16, len16, note, vel, mute, x, y, z]` sorted by t16 then note |
| `seqT.clipLane` | Float32Array(129·Σlanes) |

### 4.5 Asset entries (worklet formats; owner S1 for producers, D5 for the store)

All entries are plain objects with transferred typed arrays, delivered by `{t: 'smp', …entry}` or `processorOptions.smps`. Common fields: `id`, `v: 1`, `kind`, `bytes`. Validation in `SxBase.storeAsset` (invalid ⇒ `false`, warn once per id).

| kind | Fields |
|---|---|
| `smp` (playback) | `rate` (Hz, ≤ 48000: entries are resampled down, §9.4.3), `ch` 1/2, `len` (frames at level 0), `root` (fractional MIDI), `loop: {start, end} | null` (from the file), `levels: [{len, L: Float32Array(len), R: Float32Array(len) | null}]` 1–8 levels, level ℓ decimated by 2^ℓ (§9.4.3), `peak` |
| `spec` (analysis) | `rate`, `len`, `N`, `hop`, `frames`, `ch` 1, `mag: Float32Array(frames·(N/2+1))` (linear magnitude, window-normalised so a full-scale sine reads 1), `ph: Float32Array(frames·(N/2+1))` (principal analysis phase ∈ (−π, π]), `trans: Uint8Array(frames)` (transient flags, §9.4.6), `peak` |
| `multi` | `zones: [{lo, hi, vlo, vhi, root, tune (cents), gain (dB), pan (−1…1), smp: entry id, mode: 'oneshot'|'loop'|'sustain', ls, le (frames), off (frames), env: [delay, a, h, d, s (0–1), r] (s; null = none), rr: [seqLen, seqPos], vt (0–1 amp_veltrack; **1 when the SFZ has no `amp_veltrack`**, the SFZ default of 100 %; factory multisamples 1)}]` ≤ 512 zones (zone samples are separate `smp` entries, with levels only up to the zone's maximum upward transposition, §9.4.3) |
| `ir` (shaped) | `rate` (= the AudioContext rate it was prepared for), `ch` 1/2, `len` (shaped length), `head: [Float32Array(128) L, R]` (direct-form taps), `hp: {n, re/im: Float32Array}` per channel (FFT-256 partition spectra of IR [128, 8192)), `tp: {n, re/im: Float32Array}` per channel (FFT-8192 partition spectra of IR [8192, len)), `peak` — everything prepared in `smpwork` (op `irshape`, §8.4); the worklet only adopts the buffers |

Ids (§9.1) are opaque to the DSP. A missing entry ⇒ that oscillator/instance is silent (no throw) and becomes audible within one chunk after it arrives.

### 4.6 Versioning summary
`s.v 2`, `fx.v 2`, CP 2, FXP 2, SQP 1, asset entries 1, wavetable entries 1 (Phase 1). Main thread and DSP ship together; a version mismatch is a programming error caught by the integration tests.

---

## 5. Modulation semantics (delta on Phase-1 §5)

### 5.1 Knob space
Unchanged: eff n = clamp(base + Σ offsets, 0, 1); laws after summing; stepped laws round eff n. Applies to every destination including dynamic FX parameters and virtual destinations (whose base is supplied as described in §3.11.3).

### 5.2 Row evaluation v2 (voice, mono and FX contexts)

```
SRC[randd] := rd[row]                                  // randd (as source or aux) reads this row's own note-on draw (§6.2)
s = SRC[mSrc]                                          // native: uni [0,1] or bi [−1,1] (srcBi)
if mCrv ≥ 0:  u = uni ? s : (s+1)/2;  u = tab(CT.crv, mCrv, u);  s = uni ? u : 2u − 1     // editable curve
elif mK ≠ 0:  Phase-1 bend
if mRise > 0 or mFall > 0:                             // slew (per voice per row; mono context: per processor per row)
   R = uni ? 1 : 2;  d = s − z[row]
   z[row] += d > 0 ? min(d, R·dtC/mRise) : max(d, −R·dtC/mFall)      // a zero time = instantaneous in that direction
   s = z[row]                                          // z initialised to s at note-on (voice) / first evaluation (mono)
x = mBi ? 0.5·(uni ? 2s − 1 : s) : s                   // Phase 1
if mAux:  a = SRC[mAux] (bi → (a+1)/2), clamp;  if mAK ≠ 0: a = bend(a, mAK);  x *= mInv ? 1 − a : a
offset[mDst] += mAmt · x
```
`dtC` = chunk duration (voice) or span duration (mono, FX contexts). The OUT column graph in the UI shows `x·mAmt` of the recent voice (snapshot `mOut`, §6.20).

### 5.3 Source timing (additions)
- `env4`, `lfo9`, `lfo10`, `lfoNy` like their Phase-1 counterparts.
- `randd`: drawn per row at note-on from the voice's **second** PRNG stream `r2` (row order, §6.2), constant for the note.
- `osca/oscb/oscc/sub/filt1/filt2`: the last sample of the previous chunk's corresponding mono signal (0 at a fresh voice's first chunk).
- `actv`: computed once per span in `begin` (active voices counted at the end of the previous span).
- `vidx`, `vmod1`, `vmod2`: set at note-on from the Voice Control step (§6.16); constant for the note.
- MPE `mpex/mpey/mpez`: per voice, the voice's channel values smoothed with τ = 1.5 ms per chunk (Phase-1 performance smoothing) — §6.17.

### 5.4 Smoothing (additions)
- Base smoothing (Phase-1 two cascaded one-poles) runs over all 429 voice indices with the `mov` list optimisation; `CP.smooth = 0.01` under Disable Smoothing (≈ 60 µs: one span).
- Macro sources: one-pole τ = 6 ms × smooth on the **eff macro value** (base + mono-context offsets on `m1…m8`, §5.6).
- FX: Phase-1 FX smoothing per parameter of the flat layout.

### 5.5 Contexts (delta)
- **Voice context**: rows with `mCtx & 1`; vector `off` over 429 vi.
- **Mono context** (`begin`): rows with `mCtx & 2` → `monoOff`/`monoEff` over 429 vi; consumers: Free/Mono LFOs, mono chaos, performance destinations (`g.wheel`, `g.bend`), **macros** `m1…m8`, **sequencer** destinations `arp.*`, `clip.*` (read by SxSeq once per span, §7.3), `vc.lscale` for mono LFOs.
- **FX context** (`fxMods(proc)` after the voices): rows with `mCtx & 4` (scope `M`: `g.master`, `g.bypass`, `g.direct`, `g.bus1v`, `g.bus2v`, every dynamic FX parameter) → reused Float64Array(`MAXFX` = 8 + 48·24) of offsets in FX-ctx order; the engine passes it with `CP.fxKey` to `FXRack.setMods(arr, key)`; the rack ignores offsets whose key differs from its FXP key (an FX edit and a Serum edit compiled separately cannot mis-target).
- FX modulation from per-voice sources follows the most recent voice (Phase 1).

### 5.6 Macros as destinations (inv B17)
- `m1…m8` are scope G: rows targeting them are evaluated in the mono context. The macro **source** value per chunk = `smoothed(clamp(macros[m].v + monoOff[m_k], 0, 1))`. A macro modulated by another macro (or by itself) lags one span per hop and stays bounded by the clamp.
- Clip automation lanes (§7.5.5) and MPE→Macro mappings (§6.17) override the base `macros[m].v` while active.
- Dragging a macro tile onto another macro's knob adds a row `macroA → mB` (unipolar, amount per Phase-1 drop rule). Dropping a macro onto another macro's **label** swaps the two macros' assignments (all rows with `src`/`aux` = macroA ↔ macroB, names and values swapped) — inv B17.

### 5.7 LFO point modulation
Phase-1 §5.7 for every Normal-type LFO 1–10 (buses 1–16 unchanged). Path, Chaos and S&H types ignore point links.

### 5.8 Drag-and-drop, halos, helpers (delta)
- Drop defaults (Phase-1 §5.8) with the matrix size 64 ("Matrix full (64 slots)").
- FX bases come from `fx.racks…inst.p[pid]` (`destBase(s, fx, id)` resolves dynamic ids).
- Pref "Limit mod depth on drop" unchanged.
- **Lock Matrix** (`locks.mod` contains `matrix`): `applyPreset` keeps the current 64 rows and drops the incoming preset's rows (inv B18).
- **Apply and Delete Macros** (`applyMacros(s, fx)`): for every active row whose source is `macro1…8` and whose destination is not virtual, add its current offset at the macro's current value (`mAmt·x` per §5.2 with s = macro value, no aux, no slew) to the destination's base (clamped), then delete every row whose source or aux is a macro (inv B18).
- **Sort by Source / Destination**: stable sort of non-empty rows by `SRC_MENU2` order / destination menu order; empty rows last.
- **Create Vibrato**: Phase-1 `vibratoAssign` with 10 LFOs ("unused" = no row uses it and its mode is the default).
- **Create Velo->Amp**: Phase-1 `veloToAmp`.

---

## 6. Voice behaviour v2 (serum-dsp + sx-* scripts, normative)

### 6.1 Order inside one voice chunk (replaces Phase-1 §5.6)
1. **Advance** with the previous chunk's eff values: ENV 1–4, per-voice LFOs, per-voice chaos, portamento (Phase-1 rule; SxMod).
2. **Sources** for this chunk (SxMod): per-voice sources, global sources of chunk c (`sx.g`), audio taps of the previous chunk (§5.3), Voice Control constants.
3. **Matrix** voice rows → eff over 429 vi (modded only; the rest follow the smoothed base) (SxMod).
4. **Oscillators**: derive per-channel engine values; render SUB and NOISE first (raw + stereo); determine the oscillator order (§6.6.6); render A/B/C in that order through their type renderer (WT in serum-dsp; Sample/Multisample SxSample; Granular SxGran; Spectral SxSpec), each applying its two warp slots (§6.6). Raw per-unison-voice buffers are captured only for oscillators used as modulators.
5. **Mixer** (§6.12): filter inputs, bypass sums, Main/Direct groups, bus sends.
6. **Filters** (§6.13) in the §1 order; route their outputs.
7. **Channel gains** (ENV 1 × AMP per group, ramped) and accumulation into the processor buffers selected by `CP.outMask`.
8. **Taps**: last samples of each oscillator/sub/noise/filter for the audio sources; filter-output chunk buffers for FM/PD/AM/RM (Filter k) of the next chunk; snapshot taps (§6.20).

The first chunk after `noteOn` uses the note-on evaluation (Phase-1 §6.1) for `mOn` destinations and base values elsewhere.

### 6.2 Note-on (delta on Phase-1 §6.1)
`noteOn(n, vel01, legato, glc, ch, ex)` — `ch` = MPE member channel (1–16) or 0; `ex` = expression of a generated note or null. `n` is the integer MIDI note **after** the sequencer chain (transpose, scale, arp/clip; §7.1).

| Step | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| PRNG streams (normative, ARCH2 rule 1) | The Phase-1 voice stream `r` (re-seeded at every non-legato note-on from the processor stream, Phase-1 serum-dsp `noteOn`) keeps **exactly** its Phase-1 draws in Phase-1 order and count: rand1, rand2, chaos init, unison random table and start phases of OSC A and B, noise start. At the same re-seed point the voice seeds a second stream `r2 = xorshift((seed ^ 0x85EBCA6B) >>> 0)` (0 replaced by 1, as Phase 1 does), where `seed` is the value Phase 1 passes to `xorshift` for `r` (`sx.rng ^ (0x9E3779B9·(i + 1))`); no draw is taken from `r`. (The Phase-1 voice multiplier 0x9E3779B9 is not reused as the stream salt: for voice 0 it would make `r2` replay the processor stream.) **Every Phase-2 per-note or per-voice draw uses `r2`**: `randd`, VC u₁–u₄, OSC C's unison table and start phases, unison START (§6.7.6), multisample RAND, granular per-grain values, spectral Detune offsets, colour-noise generators, S&H LFOs. Processor-level Phase-2 randomness (mono S&H LFOs, round-robin random, the sequencer) uses processor streams other than Phase-1's `sx.rng` |
| keyboard mapping (inv B21) | for each channel X ∈ {A, B, C, N, S}: `v127 = round(127·vel01)`; `inK = lo ≤ n ≤ hi`, `inV = vlo ≤ v127 ≤ vhi`. `inV ∧ inK` → plays at n. `inV ∧ ¬inK ∧ fold` → plays at `foldInto(n, lo, hi)` (shift by octaves into the range; if no octave fits, the nearer bound). Otherwise silent for this note (channel gate 0), still rendered when `warp` is set and it is a warp source (§6.6.6). The per-channel key offset `dk_X` = played key − n is constant for the note |
| Voice Control (inv B20) | if `use & 1024`: `k = proc.vcN mod vcLen`; `proc.vcN++` (non-legato note-ons; reset by `transport(true)`); `vidx = k / vcLen`; `vmod1/2 = vcSteps[k·6 + 4/5]`. Per note offsets with fresh `r2` draws u ∈ [−1,1): `panO = steps.pan + vc.pan·u₁` (bipolar pan units), `detO = 100·(steps.det + vc.det·u₂)` cents, `cutO = 0.5·steps.cut + 0.25·vc.cut·u₃` (cutoff n offset, both filters), `envS = 2^(steps.env + vc.env·u₄)` (envelope time multiplier). `panO`/`detO` apply to channels with `vcSel` set |
| randd | only when some row has `mRd` set: one `r2` draw per matrix slot 0–63, in slot order (rows without `mRd` still consume a draw, so draws are stable when rows are edited); no draws at all otherwise |
| envelopes (inv B15) | fresh: start from the Start level (ENV 2–4 eff `eN.start`; ENV 1: 0). Steal: from the current level, unless `envStealRe` (then from the Start level). Legato (engine mono legato or poly legato): no retrigger unless `envLegInv` (then as steal) |
| poly legato (inv A17/B19 "LEGATO with MONO off is paraphonic") | The Phase-1 voice-side mechanism stays normative (Phase-1 spec §6.1 row "poly legato", serum-dsp `noteOn`): the allocator passes `legato = false`; the voice itself detects `CP.legato && !CP.mono` with another gated voice and takes R = the gated voice with the highest stamp. v2 extends what is copied from R: the stage, stage progress, level and time of **ENV 1–4** (ENV 2–4 incl. their Start/End handling), the phase, cycle counter, delay/rise elapsed time and S&H/chaos state of every per-voice **LFO 1–10** of modes RETRIG/ENVELOPE, and the portamento start pitch (R's current pitch). Envelopes with `envLegInv` restart as on a steal. Oscillator phases follow §6.4 (`phLegato`) |
| LFOs | §6.15.2 |
| oscillators | phase per §6.4 (WT); sample-type playhead init per §6.7–§6.10 (uses the `mOn` values `k.sstart`, `k.slice`, `k.mrand`, `k.timbre`, `k.ustart`, `k.uspan`, `k.mvel`) |
| MPE | the voice stores `ch`; its expression state is read from `proc.mpe` (§6.17); a stolen voice resets its expression smoothing to the channel's current values (S2 2.0.24) |
| note-on evaluation | Phase-1 rule over the extended `mOn` set; sources include `env4`, `lfo9/10`, `lfoNy`, `randd`, `vidx`, `vmod1/2`, MPE |

### 6.3 Pitch (delta on Phase-1 §6.3)

#### 6.3.1 Note pitch
`K = CP.tune[n]` (identity without tuning). Portamento (Phase-1 §6.1/§6.3) glides the voice pitch toward K. Channel X's note pitch `note'_X = glide + (CP.tune[n + dk_X] − K)` (fold offsets are tuned, constant for the note). Tracking off (eff `k.track`, `s.track`): `note'_X = 0` for Wavetable and Sub, `60` for Multisample/Sample/Granular/Spectral, without portamento.

#### 6.3.2 Bend and expression
`bendSt` = Phase-1 (master bend × eff bend up/down); the ranges are eff `g.bendup`/`g.benddn` (±24 st, v1 laws) in S1 mode and eff `g.bu2` (0…48) / `g.bd2` (−48…0) otherwise; `mpeSt = chBend·CP.mpeBend` (§6.17). Tracking off: Wavetable and Multisample still add both; Sample/Granular/Spectral add them only when `bendTrack`.

#### 6.3.3 Oscillator pitch per pitch mode (unison voice i)
```
semi : P = note' + 12·oct + semi + fine/100 + crs + stack_i + det_i + bendSt + mpeSt + tune + detO/100
harm : P = note' + 12·oct + 12·log2(h) + fine/100 + crs + stack_i + det_i + bendSt + mpeSt + tune + detO/100,
       h = semi ≥ 0 ? semi + 1 : 1/(1 − semi)          (whole-number harmonics: SEM 0 = ×1 = unchanged, 1 = ×2, −1 = ÷2)
ratio: P = Psrc + 12·log2(ratio) + fine/100 + crs + stack_i + det_i + detO/100
step : P = note' + sclSemis(oct·sclN + semi) + fine/100 + crs + stack_i + det_i + bendSt + mpeSt + tune + detO/100   (sclN = 0 ⇒ semi)
f    = a4·2^((P − 69)/12) + hz,   clamped to [0.01, 0.45·fs]
```
`semi`, `oct`, `fine`, `crs`, `ratio`, `hz` = eff values of the oscillator's destinations; `tune` = eff `g.tune`; `Psrc` = the source oscillator's pitch without unison terms (`note' + 12·oct + semi + fine/100 + crs + bendSt + mpeSt + tune` of that oscillator in its own mode) or `note' + bendSt + mpeSt + tune` for `note`. `sclSemis(d) = floor(d/sclN)·sclPer + sclSteps[mod(d, sclN)]`. The display of SEM in harm mode is `×h` (`÷k` below 0, format `semiH`), in step mode `n steps`. Harmonics examples (normative test values): semi 0 → +0 st, 1 → +12, 2 → +19.02, −1 → −12, 12 → +44.40, −12 → −44.40.
Sample-type oscillators use the playback ratio `ρ = f / (440·2^((root − 69)/12))` (root from the asset; a4 ≠ 440 shifts samples like oscillators).
Sub: `P_sub = note'_S + 12·subOct + (S2 mode: eff s.crs value) + bendSt + mpeSt + tune` (S1 mode: Phase 1).

### 6.4 Phase memory and Phase Legato (WT; inv B3)
- **S1 mode** (`CP.s1`): Phase-1 §6.4 exactly for OSC A/B — PHASE/RAND including Mem (PHASE base n ≥ 0.9995) and MemTrig (and RAND base n ≥ 0.9995) at the knob ends, and the knob shows "Mem"/"MemTrig" there; `phMem` is ignored. OSC C uses `phMem` in both modes.
- **S2 mode**: the knob-end rule does **not** exist (S2: "All Voices uses PHASE + RAND"); PHASE is 0–360° and RAND 0–100 % everywhere, and the menu selects the memory:
- `phMem = all`: start phase = PHASE offset + RAND·u (Phase-1 formula without the knob-end branch).
- `contig`: start phase `frac(memPh[o] + memSet[o·16 + i])` whatever the knobs (Phase-1 "Mem" behaviour); PHASE remains a continuous offset (a migrated Mem sound has PHASE at ≥ 359.8°, i.e. an offset within 0.2° of Phase-1's).
- `voice`: start phase `memSet[o·16 + i]` (Phase-1 "MemTrig" behaviour). Switching to `voice` while `snapshot.notes > 0` captures the recent voice's relative phases (`memNow`, Phase-1 capture path, `captureDue` generalised to 3 oscillators).
- `phLegato = 1` inverts the Phase-1 steal rule: a steal resets phases (per `phMem`); poly legato keeps them running.

### 6.5 Wavetable oscillator (delta)
- OSC C = Phase-1 OSC A/B code with `o = 2` (17-row block at vi 148, `on`/`track` at vi 165/166).
- Smooth Interpolation (`smoothWT`): a discrete entry (`interp 0`, R > 1) is read as interpolating over its R real frames (no frame-change crossfade).
- Mip level speed = product of the frequency- and phase-domain slot speeds (§3.4.1) at the chunk's amounts.

### 6.6 Warp engine (SxWarp, used by every oscillator type)

#### 6.6.1 Stages per unison voice and sample
1. **Frequency** (F slots, slot 1 then 2): `inc' = inc·g₁(m₁)·g₂(m₂)`.
2. **Phase accumulator**: `φ += inc'` (wraps in both directions; thru-zero runs backwards).
3. **Phase** (P slots in slot order): `φ₁ = W₁(φ + PHASE offset)`, `φ₂ = W₂(φ₁)`.
4. **Read**: `x(φ₂)` (WT: Hermite, mip, frames; Odd/Even reads `x(φ₂ + ½)` too). Sample types read their playhead through the intra-period mapping of §6.6.10; Spectral oscillators through their warp delay line (§6.10.7).
5. **Output** (O slots in slot order): `y = O₂(O₁(x))`. A Sync slot's window `(1 − v + v·sin²(πφ_in))` (φ_in = that slot's input phase) multiplies here.

A slot whose mode is Off, unavailable (§3.4.2), or whose modulator is unavailable (§6.6.6) is skipped. Neutral amounts (§3.4.1 last column) skip the slot exactly (bit-identical to Off) — except the base delay of a Spectral F/P-domain slot (§6.10.7, sign-off item 1).

**Block structure (normative for CPU, B18).** No per-sample cross-script call and no per-sample mode switch: per oscillator per chunk the caller asks SxWarp for whole blocks per unison voice — `incBlock` (F stage: increments × g(m) for the chunk), `phaseBlock` (P stages: φ → φ₂ for the chunk into a `Float64Array(CH)`), `outBlock` (O stages in place on the read samples) — each a monomorphic loop selected once per chunk by mode (§11.7). The caller's read loop (WT, sample, grain) only reads the precomputed buffers. Budget d (§14.1) is verified with this structure.

#### 6.6.2 Sync with Var (inv B3)
Hard sync (v = 0) = Phase-1 Sync. v = 1 = Phase-1 "Sync Window". Linear blend in between.

#### 6.6.3 Alt warps, Remap, Quantize
Phase-1 §3.3 formulas, ported verbatim into SxWarp (Remap reads `CT.remap` of the owning oscillator). serum-dsp keeps its own copies for S1 slot-1 processing (§6.21).

#### 6.6.4 FM, PD, AM, RM
- FM (true FM, inv B3.1): §3.4.1 g(m) by `osc.fm[slot]`. Sample types: `inc'` is the playhead increment (ρ scaled). Mip level speed (§6.5) uses the peak factor. Thru-Zero lets the phase/playhead run backwards; Linear clamps g ≥ 0. Spectral: §6.10.7.
- PD: φ' = frac(φ + I·m) (WT). Sample types: the read position is offset by `I·m·T₀` source frames (T₀ of §6.6.10). Spectral: §6.10.7.
- PD (Self): m = this unison voice's previous output sample (per-voice state `y₁`, cleared at a fresh note-on).
- AM, RM: Phase-1 formulas.

#### 6.6.5 Distortion and filter warps
- Distortion: `y = x + (D(x) − x)·min(1, 10w)` with D = the FX shaper of the same name at drive `d = w` (G laws Phase-1 §8.5.4; Soft Sat. §8.2). Stomp Box keeps its HP/LP states per unison voice. No oversampling beyond the processor OS.
- LPF/HPF: TPT SVF per unison voice (2 doubles each), coefficients per chunk from the unison voice frequency `f_i`; w = 0 ⇒ skipped.

#### 6.6.6 Modulators, availability, processing order
- Modulator sample m for slot sources: **o1/o2** = the other oscillator's unison voice `i mod N_other` raw output of the **same sample** (post its own warps, pre level/pan; mono average for stereo sources); **Noise** = noise raw mono; **Sub** = sub raw; **Filter k** = §6.6.7; **Self** = §6.6.4.
- Availability: an oscillator modulator must be eff-on and either in key/velocity range for this note or have `map.warp`; Noise/Sub eff-on; Filter k eff-on. Its LEVEL may be 0 and its route may be None.
- **Order and cycles** (per chunk, eff modes): edges `target ← source` for every slot of A, B, C whose source is o1/o2 (incl. spectral Vocode/Mask o1/o2, and in S1 mode the legacy slot-1 modes 18–20 after the Phase-1 single-direction rule). Edges are accepted in the order A slot 1, A slot 2, B slot 1, B slot 2, C slot 1, C slot 2; an edge that would close a cycle with accepted edges is rejected and its slot acts as Off for that chunk. Render order = topological order of the accepted edges, ties broken A, B, C.

#### 6.6.7 Filter-sourced modulation (deviation 6)
Each voice keeps `fPrev[k]` (Float64Array(CH)) = filter k's mono output (post mix, pre level) of the previous chunk; sample s of the current chunk reads `fPrev[k][s]`. Zeros on a fresh voice and while filter k is off.

#### 6.6.8 Per-unison-voice amounts
Slot 1: Phase-1 rule (`k.warp`, `k.uniwarp`); slot 2: `w = clamp(eff k.warp2 + t_i·(2·eff k.uniwarp2 − 1)·0.5, 0, 1)`. Granular: + per-grain random (§6.9.3).

#### 6.6.9 Main-thread helpers
`SxWarp.renderWarp2(frame, slots, opts) → Float32Array(2048·over)`: one cycle through both slots (`slots = [{mode, w, v, fm}, …]`, `opts = {over, remap: Float32Array(2049), mod: Float32Array(2048)|null, osc}`); used by the 2D display and Render OSC Warp (§13.3.1). Phase-1 `SerumDSP.renderWarp` stays for S1 slot-1 previews.

#### 6.6.10 Phase- and output-domain warps on sample-based oscillators (Multisample, Sample, Granular; IMPL, review A5)
A sample has no cycle phase, so the warps act on the **intra-period phase of the root period**: `T₀ = rate / f_root` source frames (`f_root = 440·2^((root − 69)/12)`; Multisample: the playing zone's sample rate and `root + tune/100`), independent of the playback ratio ρ (unison voices and grains share it). For a read position p (the playhead, a grain's position, a layer's position — after FM/PD of §6.6.4): `φ = frac(p/T₀)`, `p₀ = p − φ·T₀`.
- **P domain** (Sync, Bend ±, PWM, Asym ±, Mirror, Remap 1–4, Quantize): read at `p' = p₀ + W₂(W₁(φ))·T₀` with the WT formulas of §3.4.1 (Sync: `W = frac(φ·r)`, its Var window in stage 5); the mip level uses `|dp|`× the slot speed (§6.5).
- **O domain**: Flip uses φ (`φ < w ? x : −x`); Odd/Even reads the second sample at `p' + T₀/2`; distortion, filter, AM, RM warps act on the read sample as on wavetables.
- Reads beyond the region/loop bounds wrap or clamp as the playhead itself does (§6.7.3).
On non-periodic material the result is a periodic re-reading at the root period (stutter/formant-like effects); on tonal samples recorded at their root it reproduces the wavetable warp (test §15.8).

### 6.7 Sample oscillator (SxSample.SampleOsc; inv B6)

#### 6.7.1 Regions (positions in level-0 frames; L = entry `len`)
`S = sstart·L` (note-on value), `E = send·L` (eff, continuous). E < S ⇒ the region plays backwards from S to E. Loop markers are absolute: `LS = lstart·L`, `LE = lend·L` (eff, continuous), clamped into `[min(S,E), max(S,E)]`; `LE < LS` ⇒ the loop runs backwards. **Relative Loop**: `LS += S − S_base`, `LE += S − S_base` (S_base = sstart base). **Link Loop Length**: `LE = LS + (lend_base − lstart_base)·L`.

#### 6.7.2 Playhead rate
Per OS sample `dp = (rate/fs)·ρ·σ` with ρ from §6.3.3 (FM-modified per §6.6.4) and the scan factor `σ = (scanrev ? −1 : 1)·v·R` (v = eff `k.scan` value ∈ [−1,1], R = 2/4/8). **Sample Length to BPM** (`sLenOn`): `beats = lenLad[round(14·eff n of k.lenrate)]` (the RATE knob, modulatable, stepped), `σ = (scanrev ? −1 : 1)·(|E − S|/rate)/(beats·60/bpm)` and ρ excludes the key term (`note' − root`): the region always lasts `beats`, the pitch knobs (oct/semi/fine/crs, bend) still transpose by varispeed; `k.scan` is ignored while Length to BPM is on. **Lock Scan Rate**: `σ ×= bpm/120`. Sample type = varispeed (pitch and speed coupled; IMPL, Granular and Spectral provide time-stretching).

#### 6.7.3 Loop modes
| Mode | Behaviour |
|---|---|
| One-shot | S → E, then the channel outputs 0 (the voice lives on per ENV 1) |
| Fwd Loop | S → LE, then LS → LE repeatedly |
| Rev Loop | S → LE, then backwards LE → LS, and from LS jump to LE continuing backwards |
| Fwd/Rwd Loop | S → LE, then ping-pong between LS and LE |
| Tailed | S → E, then loop the tail: jump to M = (S + E)/2 at E with an equal-power crossfade of length `max(xfadeLen, 10 ms)` |
| + Exit Loop on Release | once the voice's gate is off, the loop is abandoned: playback continues in its current direction to the region end and stops |

#### 6.7.4 Loop crossfade
`Lx = clamp(xfade·0.5·|LE − LS|, 0, min(LS_room, |LE − LS|/2))` (LS_room = frames available before LS: S2 2.0.17 "auto-scaled inside the sample"). In the last Lx frames before the wrap point the output is `cos θ·x(p) + sin θ·x(p − (LE − LS))`, θ = (π/2)·(p − (LE − Lx))/Lx (mirrored for backward loops; ping-pong needs no crossfade).

#### 6.7.5 Slicing (inv B6)
Boundaries `b = sSlPos[o]` = `[0, marks…, 1]` over the whole sample. `sSlMode` is compiled as 0 (off) while `xyOn` (Manual X|Y, Granular/Spectral; the slicing state is kept). Normal slice mode: key n plays slice `j = n − sliceRoot` (out of range ⇒ silent) from `b_j` to `b_{j+1}` (or to E with `toEnd`), at ρ from the pitch knobs only (no keytracking); the loop menu applies within the slice. **Play Single Slice**: every key plays slice `j = clamp(round(eff k.slice), 0, count − 1)` (note-on value) with ρ following the key relative to MIDI 60. Auto slicing marks come from the asset's onset function (§9.4.5) at the threshold `thr`, computed by the panel and stored in `marks` (§2.2.3); Manual marks are edited in `marks`. The DSP only sees `sSlPos`.

#### 6.7.6 Unison START / SPAN (inv B4)
Unison voice i (detuned ρ_i, Phase-1 unison maths) starts at `S + Δ_i`, `Δ_i = (ustart·u_i + uspan·i/N)·(E − S)` (u_i ∈ [0,1) `r2` draw at note-on); past the region end it wraps into the loop (looping modes) or is silent (One-shot). The same Δ_i defines the per-stream playheads of Granular (§6.9.1) and Spectral (§6.10.1).

#### 6.7.7 Reading
Level ℓ = the smallest level with `|dp|/2^ℓ ≤ 1` (≤ 8 levels), one-chunk crossfade on a change; 4-point Hermite (Phase-1 formula) at `p/2^ℓ`; stereo entries per channel, mono feeds both. Position (eff `k.pos`) is a continuous read offset `pos·L` (scratch). `sRev`: the processed sample is the entry reversed — position p reads entry frame `L − 1 − p` (marks, loop points and slices are in processed coordinates); `sNorm`: output × `1/peak`. (Manual X|Y does not exist for the Sample type.)

### 6.8 Multisample oscillator (SxSample.MultiOsc; inv B5)
- Zone choice at note-on: lookup key `K_l = n_X − round(eff k.timbre)` (TIMBRE: positive values use zones mapped lower, played higher), velocity v127. Every zone with `lo ≤ K_l ≤ hi` and `vlo ≤ v127 ≤ vhi` plays (≤ 8 layers, entry order). Round robin: zones with `rr[0] > 1` sharing the same (lo, hi, vlo, vhi) play only when `rr[1] = (proc.rr[group] mod rr[0]) + 1`; `proc.rr[group]` increments per note-on.
- Layer playhead from `off + mrand·u·min(0.25·len, 2·rate)` (u: `r2` draw); ratio `ρ = 2^((P − (root + tune/100))/12)` with P = the oscillator pitch (§6.3.3; the played key, not K_l).
- Loop: `loop` = loop continuously over [ls, le); `sustain` = loop while the gate is on, then play to the end; `oneshot` = play to the end.
- Layer gain: `10^(gain/20)·(1 − vt·k_mvel·(1 − (v127/127)²))`, equal-power pan; layer envelope: OVERRIDE ⇒ DAHDSR from `k.mdly…k.mrel` eff values (delay in beats from `ENV_LADDER` when `msDlyBpm`); else the zone's SFZ envelope (none ⇒ gain 1 with a 1 ms release).
- Unison: every unison voice plays all layers (cost × N). Levels/reading as §6.7.7.

### 6.9 Granular oscillator (SxGran.GranOsc; inv B7)

#### 6.9.1 Playhead
As §6.7.1–§6.7.3 with σ for Granular: `σ = (scanrev ? −1 : 1)·v·R·(scanKey ? 2^((K − 60)/12) : 1)·(scanLock ? bpm/120 : 1)`; Length to BPM as Sample (σ from `k.lenrate`, §6.7.2); v = 0 freezes the playhead. The playhead does **not** carry pitch: grain pitch comes from ρ (time and pitch independent). **Per unison stream** u = 0…N−1: each stream owns a playhead that starts at `S + Δ_u` (Δ_u of §6.7.6: START random + SPAN spread) and advances by the shared σ; its grains spawn from its own playhead (and wrap/clamp per the loop mode). Manual (X|Y on): stream position = `pos·L + Δ_u` (X = the SCAN knob bound to `k.pos`, §2.2.1), no scanning, Y per §3.9, slicing off. Loop modes act on the playheads; **Loop Grains** makes each grain wrap inside [LS, LE) with the §6.7.4 crossfade.

#### 6.9.2 Spawning (per unison stream u = 0…N−1)
Interval `T_s`: Free `1/(0.1·8000^dn)` s; BPM `beats·60/bpm` (`sDensLad`); Grains `L_g/K`, `K = round(1 + 63·dn)` (dn = eff `k.dens`). Stream offsets within the interval: Together 0; Even `u/N·T_s`; Exp `(2^(u/N) − 1)·T_s`; Random `U[0,1)·T_s` redrawn per spawn. **Jump Start** (default on): at note-on each stream spawns `ceil(L_g/T_s)` grains with ages `j·T_s` (j = 0…) so the full density is immediate; off: one grain at note-on. Caps (a spawn at a cap is skipped, never a running grain cut): (1) per oscillator per voice `min(sMaxGr, qr.grainsMax, 256)`; (2) **per processor** `qr.grainsProc` (default 512, touch devices 256, §14.2) over all voices and oscillators — the processor keeps the live count `sx.grains`; when it is at the cap, spawns of every voice except the most recently triggered granular voice are skipped (the oldest voices thin out first, a new note still sounds), and that voice spawns only up to its own cap. Jump Start spawns count against both caps. (Budget: 8 voices × 3 Granular oscillators stays ≤ 2 × the CPU of row g′, §14.1.)

#### 6.9.3 Per-grain values (drawn at spawn from the voice's `r2` stream; u = U[0,1))
| Value | Formula |
|---|---|
| length | Free `0.001·10^(4·ln)` s; BPM ladder; Percent `(800^ln/100)·T_s` (ln = eff `k.glen`; Percent with DENS Grains ⇒ Free); × `2^(rlen·(2u−1))` |
| start | playhead + `roff·(2u−1)·0.5·|E − S|`, folded into the region |
| direction | reverse ⇔ `(u < 0.5·rdir) XOR grev` |
| pitch | `ρ_u·2^(rpitch·(2u−1)/12)` |
| pan | stream pan + `rpan·(2u−1)`, clamped to [−1,1], equal-power |
| level | `1 − rlvl·u` |
| window | amount `a = clamp(wamt + rwamt·(2u−1))`, skew `s = clamp(skew + rwskew·(2u−1), −1, 1)` (skew = eff `k.wskew` value) |
| F/P-domain amounts | slot amounts + `rwarp·(2u−1)` (slot 1), `rwarp2·(2u−1)` (slot 2), clamped; FM/PD and the phase-domain warps (§6.6.10) act per grain on its read position; output-domain warps are applied per stream and ignore Rand Warp (IMPL) |
| overlap gain | `1/√max(1, L_g/T_s)` |

#### 6.9.4 Window shaping
`Wg(t) = 1 − a + a·W(t_s)`, t ∈ [0,1] grain age fraction; `t_s = s ≥ 0 ? t^(1/(1+3s)) : 1 − (1−t)^(1/(1+3|s|))`; W from §3.8 (table lookup).

#### 6.9.5 Rendering
Each grain reads its own level (`|dp_g|` rule) with Hermite, `y = Wg·level·overlap·x`, panned into its stream's stereo sum; streams get the Phase-1 unison gains/pans/blend; raw mono per stream for modulators. Grain state: `Float64Array(256·16)` per oscillator per voice, preallocated on the first `apply` that sets a Granular type.

### 6.10 Spectral oscillator (SxSpec.SpecOsc; inv B8)

#### 6.10.1 Streams and rates
One phase-vocoder stream per unison voice (each with its own ρ_i and START/SPAN playhead `S + Δ_i`, §6.7.6; Manual X|Y: `pos·L + Δ_i`, slicing off). Synthesis runs at `proc.base` with `N_s = SPEC_N(base)`, hop `H_s` per quality (§3.9), Hann analysis/synthesis windows, overlap-add normalisation `1/Σ w²`; the stream output is upsampled to `proc.fs` by the SxBase half-band interpolator (one stage per OS doubling).
- **Stream pool (memory, B9)**: stream state (FFT scratch, OLA buffer, phase/magnitude state, warp states, upsampler) lives in a **processor-level pool** of `qr.specStreams` streams (default 32; 16 on touch devices, §14.2), allocated in `apply` when some oscillator is Spectral (never in `new SpecOsc`, which only holds indices). A voice's oscillator takes free streams at note-on (one per unison voice) and returns them when the voice ends; with the pool empty the remaining unison voices are not rendered (snapshot `specCap`).
- **Staggered FFTs (CPU bursts, B9)**: frame k of a stream is synthesised **during hop k − 1** (one hop ahead: its control values — pitch, playhead, warps — are taken one hop early; no audio latency), in the chunk whose index within the hop is `((streamIdx·CH) mod H_s)/CH`, so streams started together spread their IFFTs over the hop.
- **Pre-roll** (`N_s/H_s − 1` frames needed for a full overlap-add) is synthesised across the chunks of the first hop after note-on (at most one frame per chunk per stream), and the stream's output is faded in over that first hop (raised cosine). The onset of a Spectral note is therefore soft over ≤ one hop (IMPL).

#### 6.10.2 Analysis reading
The playhead P (analysis frames, fractional) advances `σ·H_s·(srate/base)/hop_a` per output frame (σ as Granular §6.9.1). Magnitudes: linear interpolation between frames ⌊P⌋, ⌈P⌉. Instantaneous frequency of analysis bin k: `κ_k = k + princ(ph[t+1,k] − ph[t,k] − 2π·k·hop_a/N_a)·N_a/(2π·hop_a)` (bins), `F_k = κ_k·srate/N_a`.

#### 6.10.3 Resynthesis with pitch
Output bin j (`f_j = j·base/N_s`): source position `b = (f_j/ρ)·N_a/srate`; `M_j = lerp(mag[⌊b⌋], mag[⌈b⌉])`; output frequency `F_out = ρ·F_{round(b)}`; phase `φ_j += 2π·F_out·H_s/base`. **Phase Lock**: local maxima of M are peaks; every bin in a peak's region (to the minima on either side) takes `φ_j = φ_peak + π·(j − peak)`. **Transients**: on a frame flagged transient (`trans`) phases are reset to `φ_j = ph[⌊P⌋, round(b)]`.

#### 6.10.4 Processing order per synthesis frame
(1) Lo/Hi band limit unless Post Warp; (2) spectral filter: `x = log(f/20)/log(1000)`, `x' = x − 0.5·cutV`, `M ×= 1 − mix + mix·specFilt(x')` (cutV = eff `k.cut` value, mix = eff `k.smix`); (3) spectral warp slots 1, 2; (4) Lo/Hi band limit if Post Warp; (5) inverse FFT, window, overlap-add; (6) after upsampling: F/P-domain warps through the warp delay line (§6.10.7), then O-domain warps (LPF/HPF, distortions, Flip, Odd/Even, AM, RM) per stream. Band limit: gain 1 inside [flo, fhi], 0 outside; **Smooth**: `1/√(1 + (flo/f)^8) · 1/√(1 + (f/fhi)^8)`.

#### 6.10.5 Spectral warps (w = slot amount; `N_b = N_s/2 + 1`; M, Φ as above)
| Mode | Operation |
|---|---|
| Comb | period `P = 2 + 62(1 − w)` bins, depth `d = min(1, 2w)`: `M_j ×= 1 − d·(0.5 − 0.5·cos(2πj/P))` |
| Gate | `thr = w²·max M`; `M_j = M_j ≥ thr ? M_j : 0` |
| Add Harmonics | `M'_j = M_j + w·(0.5·M_{j/2}[j even] + 0.33·M_{j/3}[3 | j])` |
| Add Subharmonics | `M'_j = M_j + w·(0.5·M_{2j} + 0.33·M_{3j})` (indices < N_b) |
| Spread | `M'_j = M(j/(1 + 2w))`, frequencies scaled by `(1 + 2w)` (inharmonic stretch) |
| Smear | per-bin state `Z_j ← Z_j + (M_j − Z_j)·(1 − 0.97w)`; `M'_j = Z_j` |
| Detune | per-bin offsets `r_j ∈ [−1,1)` drawn from `r2` at note-on; bin frequency × `2^(w·r_j·50/1200)` |
| Peak Octave Up / Down | peaks above `max M·10^(−2)`: add `w·M_p` at bin `2p` / `p/2` with frequency × 2 / × ½ |
| Peak Harm Up / Down | as above with 3p / p/3 |
| Shepard Filter / Narrow | `g_j = 0.5 + 0.5·cos(2π(log2(f_j/20) − w))`; Narrow `g_j⁶`; `M ×= g` |
| Vocode (B/C/Noise) | E = 24 one-third-octave band envelopes (linear interpolation across bands) of the modulator spectrum (a Spectral modulator: its current frame; otherwise an STFT of its raw output over the last N_s samples **at base rate** — the OS-rate modulator signal is decimated by `SxBase.HBDown` into a per-stream base-rate ring before the STFT) and of this spectrum: `M'_j = min(16·M_j, M_j·(1 − w + w·E_mod/max(1e-9, E_self)))` |
| Mask (B/C/Noise) | `M'_j = M_j·(1 − w + w·min(1, 4·Mmod_j / max Mmod))` |
| Mirror | pivot `q = round(N_b·(0.02 + 0.48w))`; for j < 2q: `M'_j = M_{2q−j}` |
| Pitch Shift | `r = 2^((w − 0.5)·4)` (±24 st): `M'_j = M(j/r)`, frequencies × r |
| Pitch Blend | `M' = (1 − w)·M + w·shift₊₁₂(M)` |
| Shift | `Δ = (2w − 1)·N_b/4` bins: `M'_j = M_{j−Δ}`, frequencies + `Δ·base/N_s` Hz |
| Phase Twist | `Φ_j += w·32π·(j/N_b)²` per frame |

#### 6.10.6 Spectral filter from a wavetable
"Use Wavetable as Filter" (UI) stores `spec.filt = {kind: 'wt', wt: id, pts: frameToPoints(frame 0 mapped y = 0.5 + 0.5·x, 128)}`; the DSP only sees the compiled curve.

#### 6.10.7 Frequency- and phase-domain warps on Spectral oscillators (IMPL, review A5; sign-off item 1)
The resynthesised stream (OS rate, after upsampling) has no read phase, so F/P-domain slots act through a per-stream **warp delay line** (Float32, ≤ 50 ms at the OS rate, 4-point Hermite reads), active whenever a slot of the oscillator has an F- or P-domain mode (not only when its amount is non-zero, so amount changes never jump the delay):
- Base delay `D₀ = min(25 ms, 2·T₀)` fixed at note-on, `T₀ = 1/f_i` of that unison voice's note-on frequency. Output `y(t) = x(t − D₀ + Δ(t))`, `Δ` clamped to `±(D₀ − 2 samples)`; Δ sums the slots in slot order:
  - **P domain** (Sync, Bend, PWM, Asym, Mirror, Remap, Quantize): `φ = frac(θ(t))` with θ the stream's running fundamental phase (∫ f_i dt, reset at note-on), `Δ = (W(φ) − φ)·T₀(t)`; Odd/Even's second read and Flip use the same φ; a Sync slot's Var window multiplies the output.
  - **PD**: `Δ = I·m·T₀(t)` (I as §3.4.1), i.e. `y = x(t − D₀ + I·m·T₀)`.
  - **FM**: `Δ = z(t)`, `z` = the running integral of `g(m) − 1` (seconds) through a one-pole leak at 5 Hz (so Exp FM's mean pitch rise is not accumulated without bound), clamped as above.
- The line and its 10 ms crossfade when an F/P mode is selected or cleared (from undelayed to delayed output) are per stream (pool, §6.10.1). A selected F/P slot thus delays the Spectral oscillator by D₀ even at amount 0 — the documented exception to §0.7.

### 6.11 Sub and noise (delta)

#### 6.11.1 Sub (inv B9)
Shape = S1 mode `SUB_IDS[eff s.shape]`, else `SUB_IDS2[eff s.shp]`. Level gain: S1 mode `n` (Phase 1), S2 mode `n²`. Start phase (fresh voice or reset) = eff `s.phase`/360; `subContig`: the phase continues from the processor's last sub phase (`sx.subPh`). Pitch §6.3.3. Tracking off ⇒ MIDI 0.

#### 6.11.2 Colour noise (`noiseColor ≥ 0`; inv B10)
Two independent xorshift streams a, b (white ±1) seeded from `r2` at note-on (§6.2). Pink: Paul Kellet's 7-pole refined filter per stream (coefficients 0.99886/0.0555179, 0.99332/0.0750759, 0.96900/0.1538520, 0.86650/0.3104856, 0.55000/0.5329522, −0.7616/−0.0168980, white × 0.5362, + previous white × 0.115926), output × 0.11. Brown: `y = 0.998·y + 0.0414·w`, output `3.5·y`. Geiger: Poisson clicks at `λ = 40·2^(pitchSt/12)` /s (pitchSt = eff `n.pitch` value), each click `±(0.5 + 0.5u)` decaying with τ = 0.15 ms. All colours are scaled to −12 dBFS RMS (Phase-1 colour level). STEREO st (eff `n.stereo`): `L = a`, `R = ((1 − st)·a + st·b)/√((1 − st)² + st²)`. FILTER c (eff `n.color` value): c > 0 one-pole LP at `20000·2^(−9c)` Hz; c < 0 one-pole HP at `20·2^(9|c|)` Hz; c = 0 none. PITCH (except Geiger), START, RAND, keytrack are ignored for colours.

#### 6.11.3 Sample noise
Phase-1 §6.8 unchanged.

### 6.12 Mixer (per voice; inv B1, B13)
For each channel X (A, B, C, Noise, Sub) after its own level/pan and keyboard gate:

```
r = route[X]
r = Filter: b = eff X.fbal value; s1 = (1 − b)/2; s2 = (1 + b)/2
            G_k = ramp( eff fk.on ≥ ½  ∧  eff fk.route<X> ≥ ½ )            (one-chunk linear ramp, Phase 1)
            u_k += s_k·G_k·x ;  v_k += s_k·(1 − G_k)·x                       (k = 1, 2)
r = Main:   main[envOn[X]] += x        r = Direct: direct[envOn[X]] += x        r = None: —
sends:      bus_j[r = Filter ? 1 : envOn[X]] += x·sendGain_j(X)                 (sendGain = (eff X.busj n)²)
```
Filter channels (k = 1, 2): `y_k = L_k·((1 − mix_k)·u_k + mix_k·F_k(u_k) + v_k)`, `L_k` = `(2n)²` of eff `f.level`/`f2.level`; Combs (type 80) forces mix 1 (both filters). Then `route[F_k]` (other filter: `u_other += y_k`; Main/Direct/None with `envOn[F_k]`) and sends `fK.bus1/2`.
Channel gains: `amp = (eff g.amp)²`, `e` = ENV 1; every group gets `amp·(env ? e : 1)`, ramped linearly per sample across the chunk from the previous chunk's target (Phase-1 ramp). Each of main, direct, bus1, bus2 has an env-on and an env-off group.
**Summation order** (normative, S1 equivalence): u₁ and v₁ sum A, B, Noise, Sub, C; Direct sums Sub, Noise, A, B, C; Main A, B, C, Noise, Sub, F1, F2. In S1 mode the Filter-1 channel output is computed as `v₁ + u₁ + mix·(F(u₁) − u₁)` when `L₁ = 1` (Phase-1 arithmetic form).

### 6.13 Filters (delta; inv B12)

#### 6.13.1 Instances
Two `SFilter`s per voice; Filter 2's memory is allocated lazily (first `apply` with `use & 2`). `SFilter` keeps the Phase-1 API; for t ≥ 96 it holds a `SxFilt2` family object created **only in `apply`/`prealloc`** (the Phase-1 rule: a modulated type preallocates the families its rows can reach); `setType(t)` on the render path never allocates — a family that was not preallocated passes audio unfiltered for that chunk and is requested for the next `apply` (one `console.warn`). `tick`/`process` delegate to the family object; `prealloc(all)` covers 0–107. Per SFilter per `setXY`/`setPZ` call (once per chunk) the PZ SVF corners and X/Y are passed (§11.7).
- **Memory (B12, normative)**: every type 0–107 ≤ 2 MB per voice (both filters) at base 192 kHz. Delay-based new families (Diffusor, Comb 2) use Float32 lines with a **delay floor of 20 Hz** (`fc` and Comb 2's `f2` clamped to ≥ 20 Hz for line sizing and tuning), ≤ 1 MB per filter at 192 kHz (Diffusor: Σ_j d_j ≤ 0.75·fs/20 frames × 2 channels × 4 bytes).

#### 6.13.2 Type and controls
Filter 1 type = eff `f.type` (S1 mode) or eff `f.type2`; Filter 2 = eff `f2.type`. Cutoff/res/drive/var/pan/mix laws and the Phase-1 §6.11 behaviour apply to both (f2.* destinations), with the per-voice VC cutoff offset `cutO` added to both cutoff n before the law. X/Y (`fK.x`, `fK.y`) only for PZ SVF.

#### 6.13.3 Clean Mode
Input × 10^(−24/20), output × 10^(24/20) around the family's processing (`filter[k].clean`).

#### 6.13.4 PZ SVF
A 2-pole TPT SVF (Phase-1 resonance law) whose output is `cl·lp + cb·bp + ch·hp`, with `(cl, cb, ch)` = bilinear interpolation of the four corners `pz` at (X, Y) = eff (`fK.x`, `fK.y`): `TL·(1−X)(1−Y) + TR·X(1−Y) + BL·(1−X)Y + BR·XY`.

#### 6.13.5 New family recipes (SxFilt2; IMPL, level-calibrated per Phase-1 §6.11: LP/HP/BP passband 1 ± 1 dB at res 0, drive 0)
| Family | Recipe |
|---|---|
| `wasp` | 2-pole SVF with integrator saturation `s(x) = x/(1 + |x|)` on each state update; MORPH m = var: `m < ½ ? lerp(lp, notch, 2m) : lerp(notch, hp, 2m − 1)`, notch = lp + hp; asymmetric input bias `+0.05·drive` (buzz) |
| `djm` | position `c = log(fc/MIDI0)/log(22050/MIDI0)`: c < 0.49 → 2-pole LP at `20·1000^(2c/0.98)`; c > 0.51 → 2-pole HP at `20·1000^((c − 0.51)/0.49)`; 0.49–0.51 → linear crossfade with the dry input (flat at 0.5); resonance per Phase-1 SVF law |
| `diffusor` | `S = 1 + round(15·var)` Schroeder allpasses in series, delays `d_j = (fs/fc)·0.5^(j/16)·(1 + 0.13·j mod 3)` samples (j = 0…S−1), gain `g = 0.3 + 0.6·res`; output = chain output (MIX blends) |
| `ladder2` | 4-pole ladder (Phase-1 ladder family, tanh per stage, k = 4.1·res, no FAT) with cutoff smoothing: `log2 fc` one-pole per chunk, τ = `0.05·var²` s (SMOOTH). Acid: diode ladder — stage coupling `y_k += g·(y_{k−1} − y_k + 0.5·(y_{k+1} − y_k))` (k = 1…4, y₀ = input − feedback), feedback `k = 6·res`, output y₄·2. EMS: linear ladder (no tanh), feedback path through a one-pole HP at fc/4, k = 3.6·res |
| `dirty` | MG Ladder with input gain `1 + 15·var²` (PAIN) and `tanh(2·x)` in the feedback |
| `pzsvf` | §6.13.4 + SMOOTH (var) as `ladder2` |
| `comb2` | two feedback combs in series at `fc` and `f2 = fc·2^(4(var − 0.5))` (FRQ2), feedback `0.9995·res` each with `1.5·tanh(x/1.5)` in the loop, output scaled `√(1 − fb²)` |
| `expmm` | clean 2-pole SVF; Exp MM: MIX var blends LP → Notch → HP as `wasp`; Exp BPF: BP output (unity peak) |
| `k35` | Korg-35 TPT LP (two one-poles + HP feedback), feedback `k = 2·res` through `tanh` |

#### 6.13.6 Key track
S1 mode: Phase-1 (`nP`, the gliding voice pitch). S2 mode (inv B12): the note' of the first oscillator in A, B, C order whose eff track is on and which is on, including portamento and fold; none ⇒ the played MIDI note (no glide). Factor `2^((ref − 60)/12)`.

### 6.14 Envelopes (delta; inv B15)
- ENV 4 = ENV 2 code; ENV 2–4 Start/End: attack starts at the Start level S₀ (fresh) — `v = S₀ + (1 − S₀)·bend(q, kA)`; release targets the End level E₀ — `v = E₀ + (vR − E₀)·(1 − bend(q, kR))`, after release the output holds E₀ (the voice-lifetime test uses ENV 1 only, which has neither).
- **BPM units** (`envBpm`): A/H/D/R eff n map to `ENV_LADDER[round(n·15)]` beats → seconds at `proc.bpm` (stepped). Switching units in the UI converts the base value to the nearest entry at the current tempo (inv B15).
- Times × `envS` (VC, §6.2) × eff `vc.escale` value — each factor skipped at its neutral value (§0.7).
- Phase-1 curve laws unchanged. Start/End display in dB (`db` format; 0 = `-inf dB`, inv B15 default).
- The editor's **Grid ▸ Time / Beats** (inv B15) only changes the grid lines and snapping of the envelope display (ms grid or beat grid at the current tempo); stored in `ui.envGrid` (0 Time, 1 Beats).

### 6.15 LFOs (delta; inv B16; SxMod)

#### 6.15.1 Modes and MONO
| mode | mono 0 (per voice) | mono 1 (processor) |
|---|---|---|
| RETRIG | Phase-1 Trig | one phase in `begin`, restarted at every note-on of the processor; delay/rise measured from that note-on |
| ENVELOPE | Phase-1 Env | one phase in `begin` with Env semantics, restarted at every note-on; the loop holds while any voice is gated |
| FREE | phase from the processor clock (Phase-1 Off-mode phase), evaluated per voice (per-voice bus values, delay/rise/smooth per voice) | Phase-1 Off mode exactly |

HOST (`anchor`): BPM on — Phase-1 Anchor (now also for mono LFOs); BPM off — **S2 mode only**: while the transport plays, RETRIG/ENVELOPE start at `φ = frac(start + songSeconds·rateHz)` and FREE follows `φ = frac(start + songSeconds·rateHz)` (reproducible); off — free accumulation (Phase-1). **S1 mode**: HOST has no effect with BPM off (Phase-1 free accumulation, serum-dsp `offLfo`; v1 LFOs default to `anchor = 1`, §6.21 item 5). `songSeconds` = `songBeat()·60/bpm`.

#### 6.15.2 Start phase and direction
Start phase = `pts[start].x` when eff `lN.phase` = 0 exactly (Phase-1 value, which may be 1 — an Env-mode LFO starting on its last point holds there; no `frac`), else `frac(pts[start].x + eff lN.phase/360)`. Direction: Forward reads φ; Reverse reads `1 − φ`; Ping Pong reads φ on even cycles and `1 − φ` on odd cycles (the cycle counter is part of the LFO state; RETRIG resets it). Rate: Hz law ×10 when `x10` (0–1000 Hz); BPM ladder Phase 1; × the effective LFO scale (eff `vc.lscale` value, snapped to `VC_RATES` when `vcLMode = 1`; skipped at 1). Swing (BPM on, `lfoSwing`): with s = `kbd.swing` ∈ [−1, 1], even cycles last ×`(1 + 0.75s)` and odd cycles ×`(1 − 0.75s)` of the unswung cycle (same pair geometry as §7.2). The PHASE knob menu's **Snap to Grid** quantises the base PHASE to multiples of `360/lfoGrid.x` degrees while dragging (UI).

#### 6.15.3 Types
- **Normal**: Phase 1 (graph, point modulation).
- **Path**: outputs `x = pathX[φ]`, `y = pathY[φ]` (CT tables: the polyline/curve of `lfo.path` resampled to 1025 points at equal arc length, segment curvature by Phase-1 `bend`); source `lfoN` = x, `lfoNy` = y.
- **Chaos: Lorenz / Rossler**: Phase-1 chaos integrator (§6.13 Phase 1) with `rateHz` from the LFO rate (BPM: cycles/s of the ladder entry); output `clamp(0.5 + x/40, 0, 1)` (Lorenz) / `clamp(0.5 + x/24, 0, 1)` (Rossler); seeded per voice (mono: per processor); the graph is ignored.
- **S&H**: at every cycle wrap (and at a retrigger) the output takes a new uniform value (voice `r2`; mono: a Phase-2 processor stream, §6.2); SMOOTH glides between values.
- DELAY/RISE/SMOOTH apply to every type; Y uses the same DELAY/RISE/SMOOTH.

### 6.16 Voice Control (inv B20)
§6.2 defines the per-note values. `vc.*` destinations are `V*` (sampled at note-on except `vc.lscale`, which is `V*M`). LFO SCALING mode (`vc.lmode`): % → the eff `vc.lscale` value; Rate → the `VC_RATES` entry nearest to it in log2 (ties to the smaller), constant between modulation steps. The VC preset menu lists factory VC presets (§10.9) and user presets (`synth.sx.vc`).

### 6.17 MPE (inv B24.4b)
- Engine state `proc.mpe` = Float64Array(17·4): `[ch·4 + 0]` per-channel pitch bend (−1…1), `+1` CC74/127 (Y), `+2` channel pressure/127 (Z), `+3` **CC10 as X** (−1…1: value `cc ≤ 64 ? (cc − 64)/64 : (cc − 64)/63`, so 0 → −1, 64 → 0, 127 → +1; initial 0); updated by `{t: 'mpe', ch, k: 'b'|'y'|'z'|'x', v}` (§12.4). Channel 1 is the master channel (lower zone): its bend and pressure act as the Phase-1 global bend/aftertouch; CC10 on channel 1 is ignored for MPE.
- Per voice with member channel `ch`: `mpeSt = bend_ch·CP.mpeBend`; source `mpex` = `CP.mpeBendX ? bend_ch : x_ch` (bipolar; S2: "By default, Serum maps … CC10 (pan) messages to note expression X"; the preference "MPE Pitch Bend (also) maps to Expr X" takes X from the per-note bend instead); `mpey` = Y (or `2Y − 1` when `srcBi` marks it bipolar, app preference `mpeYBi` "Expr Y acts bi-directional"); `mpez` = Z. Smoothed per chunk (τ 1.5 ms). Voices without a channel (`ch = 0`) take X/Y/Z from the generated note's expression `ex` (§7.1) when present, else 0.
- `global.mpe.xyz` compiles to `CP.mpeMac` (1: macros 1/2/3 ← X as (x+1)/2, Y, Z; 2: macros 1/2 ← Y, Z) and `CP.mpeWheelY` (3: the voice's `wheel` source ← its Y). Mapped macros are **per voice**: the macro source of that voice is its expression value (V scope); the mono context uses the recent voice's values.
- MPE off (`CP.mpeOn = 0`): channels are ignored, Phase-1 behaviour.

### 6.18 Tuning (inv B24.4)
`CP.tune[n]` = fractional MIDI pitch of key n (relative to A4 = 440 Hz; the concert-pitch knob still scales the result by `a4/440`); `NaN` for keys a `.kbm` leaves unmapped (`x`) — the allocator ignores those note-ons. Computed by `tuning.js` (§11.4) from `.scl` (+ optional `.kbm`) or `.tun` (AnaMark v1/v2). Affects oscillators, sub, keytracked sample noise (`(K − root)`), filter key track, NOTE source (`note.porta` path uses the tuned pitch/127), portamento. The NOTE curve **always** uses the tuned pitch; there is no option (inv B17/C18 "option whether tuning applies to the Note curve" [low, 2.1.4 claim] — decided, Appendix B). MTS-ESP: not available (deviation 1).

### 6.19 Voice lifetime
A voice becomes inactive when it is not gated and: (a) every audible channel has its ENV toggle on (or routes through filters) and ENV 1 ≤ 1e-5 and the channel gains have ramped to ≤ 1e-5 (Phase 1); or (b) some audible channel has its ENV toggle off — all four envelopes are idle (stage 0 or holding End ≤ 1e-5) — inv B13 "terminates after the longest release of any of the envelopes". Grains still sounding after (a)/(b) are cut with a 2 ms fade.

### 6.20 Snapshot v2 (delta on Phase-1 §8.4.2)
New fields (reused object, arrays copied into `smon`): `eff: Float32Array(429 + NFX)` (vi order, then FX-ctx eff), `lfoPh/lfoOut: Float32Array(10)`, `lfoY: Float32Array(10)`, `env/envStage/envT: (4)`, `wtPos: Float32Array(3)`, `smpPos: Float32Array(3·4)` (per osc: playhead, S, LS, LE as fractions — animated markers), `grains: Uint16Array(3)`, `specCap` (0/1), `memNow: Float32Array(48)`, `filtTap: Float32Array(2·1024)` (F1 then F2), `mix: Float32Array(11)` (channel peak dBFS over the last span: A, B, C, N, S, F1, F2, Bus1, Bus2, Main, Direct; voices add their channel peaks, the rack adds bus/main/direct), `mOut: Float32Array(64)` (per row `mAmt·x` of the recent voice), `zone: Int16Array(3)` (multisample: zone index of the recent voice's first layer), `seq: Float64Array(16)` (§7.10), `oscVoices` (incl. grains), `maxVoices`, `notes`, `tapRate`.

### 6.21 S1 mode (`CP.s1 = 1`) — the complete list of S1-specific behaviour
1. Warp slot 1 of OSC A/B uses the v1 destination `a.warpMode`/`b.warpMode` with the Phase-1 23-mode list, formulas, speeds, windows and single-direction rule, on the Phase-1 code path in serum-dsp; `a.w1`/`b.w1` are ignored. OSC C and every slot 2 use WARPS2.
2. Sub shape from `s.shape` (5 shapes); sub level law `n` (linear).
3. Filter 1 type from `f.type` (0–95).
4. Filter key track reference = the gliding voice pitch (Phase 1).
5. LFOs with BPM off ignore HOST (Phase-1 free accumulation; the S2 Hz-HOST anchoring of §6.15.1 is off).
6. OSC A/B start phases use the Phase-1 knob-end rule (PHASE ≥ 0.9995 ⇒ Mem, + RAND ≥ 0.9995 ⇒ MemTrig, knob text "Mem"/"MemTrig"); `phMem` of A/B is ignored (§6.4).
7. Pitch-bend ranges from `g.bendup`/`g.benddn` (±24, v1 laws) instead of `g.bu2`/`g.bd2` (§6.3.2).
8. Nothing else. In particular every v2 feature works in S1 mode, and S1-variant FX instances are independent of the flag (they are a per-instance property, §3.16.2). Behaviours that are identical in both modes because their v2 form is neutral for v1 states (start phase at `lN.phase` = 0, §6.15.2; every term of §0.7) are not listed here.

With S1 mode, only v1 destinations used, OSC C, Filter 2, warp slot 2, buses, sequencer, MPE, tuning and VC inactive, the voice output MUST equal the Phase-1 output within 1e-6 absolute per sample (§15.1), including during song playback with transport play/seek/loop (the golden script's transport segment).

---

## 7. Sequencer: keyboard, arpeggiator, clip player (SxSeq + seq-core; owner D6)

### 7.1 Pipeline
When `seq.active` (§4.4), every note event for the processor — live `on`/`off`, song notes, on-screen keyboard, auditions, MIDI OUT events from another track (§7.9) — enters `proc.sxSeq.input(on, n, vel, ch, F)` instead of the voice allocator. Without SxSeq (load failure) the engine applies `kbd.trans` itself and allocates directly. Chain (normative):

```
input ─► trigger octaves (raw MIDI note; only for a module whose eff on is set): arp slot launch / clip launch, consumed
      ─► keyboard TRANSPOSE (kbd.trans) ─► KEY/SCALE quantise
      ─► clip player: when clip.on ∧ kb ≠ Off the note is consumed as a clip trigger/transposer (§7.5.4); else passes
      ─► arpeggiator: when arp.on ∧ the note is inside the ARP key/vel range: held by the arp (and passed on as well with THRU); else passes
      ─► generated notes (clip, arp): + clip/arp transpose, KEY/SCALE quantise
      ─► voice allocator (§12.3)                     MIDI OUT taps: Clip Player = clip output before the arp; On = final output
```
Clip output feeds the arpeggiator (S2: "clip → arp → key/scale"). Generated notes carry `gen = 1` and optional expression `x, y, z` (clip note lanes / arp X lane) used by the voice when it has no MPE channel.

### 7.2 Timebase and swing
- **Transport playing**: beat position `b(F) = tickAt(F)/960` (engine). **Stopped**: a free clock `sq.beat` advancing at `proc.bpm`; it is reset to 0 when the sequencer goes from idle (no playing slot, no held note, no sounding generated note) to busy, so a pattern starts on its grid at the first key press. A transport play-with-seek re-anchors running arps/clips (§7.8).
- **Event times** are exact frames; the engine splits spans at sequencer events (granularity 4 base frames, as song events).
- **Swing** (`kbd.swing` s ∈ [−1, 1], division d = 0.5/0.25/0.125 beats): inside each pair `[2kd, 2kd + 2d)` positions are remapped piecewise-linearly so the pair midpoint moves from `2kd + d` to `2kd + d·(1 + 0.75·s)` (s = ±1 puts the offbeat at 0.875 / 0.125 of the pair; negative swing pulls it early). Applied to arp step times, clip note starts and ends, and swung LFOs (§6.15.2). Display (UI): `50 + 37.5·s %` = 12.5–87.5 % (Live's convention, inv B21; "OFF" only at 0).

### 7.3 Parameters
Once per span the sequencer reads the mono-context offsets of `arp.*`/`clip.*` (previous span, `sx.monoOff`), adds them to the playing slot's/clip's base `n.*`, clamps and applies the destination law (§3.11). `arp.on`/`clip.on` use their eff values (turning one off releases its generated notes at once).

### 7.4 Arpeggiator (inv B22)

#### 7.4.1 Note order per SHAPE (H = held notes; sorted ascending except Played = arrival order; N = |H|; indices 0…N−1)
| Shape | Index sequence of one pass |
|---|---|
| Played | 0…N−1 in arrival order |
| Up / Down | 0…N−1 / N−1…0 |
| UpDown / DownUp | 0…N−1, N−2…1 / N−1…0, 1…N−2 |
| Up&Down / Down&Up | 0…N−1, N−1…0 / N−1…0, 0…N−1 |
| Converge | 0, N−1, 1, N−2, … (outer → inner) |
| Diverge | Converge reversed (inner → outer) |
| Conv&Div | Converge then Diverge without repeating the middle element |
| Thumb Up | 0, 1, 0, 2, …, 0, N−1 (N = 1: 0) |
| Thumb UD | 0 interleaved with UpDown of 1…N−1 |
| Chord | all N at every step |
| Rand / Rand NoDup | uniform index per step / uniform excluding the previous index |
| Rand Once | a permutation drawn at every restart, repeated |
| Rand Drift | random walk ±1 per step, reflected at the ends |
| Pattern | §7.4.3 |

#### 7.4.2 Transpose range (SHIFT s semitones, RANGE R passes, RANGE shape)
Pass index sequences: Up 0…R−1 · Down R−1…0 · Up/Down 0…R−1, R−2…1 · Down/Up R−1…0, 1…R−2 · Up+Down 0…R−1, R−1…0 · Down+Up R−1…0, 0…R−1 · Thumb Up 0, 1, 0, 2, … · Thumb UD thumb over Up/Down · Pinky Up R−1, 0, R−1, 1, … · Pinky UD · Converge 0, R−1, 1, R−2, … · Diverge (reversed Converge) · Con+Diverge · Chord (all passes sound at once at every step) · Random · Rnd.NoDup · Rnd.Drift · Rnd.Once (as §7.4.1). A pass = one complete §7.4.1 sequence; the note sounds `r·s` semitones above its held pitch (+ eff `arp.trans`). OFFSET rotates the §7.4.1 sequence. REPEATS = number of complete (shape × range) cycles before the arpeggiator stops until the next retrigger (0 = endless).

#### 7.4.3 Pattern (SHAPE = Pattern)
The pattern clock counts sixteenths at **one pattern sixteenth per RATE division**. Events `[t16, len16, row, vel, acc]` fire when the playhead crosses t16. Row r > 0 → index r−1 from the bottom of H, r < 0 → |r|−1 from the top. Index ≥ N: **Wrap** → index `(r−1) mod N` transposed by `floor((r−1)/N) mod (wrange + 1)` × `wtrans` semitones; **Phantom** → silent; **Clamp** → highest (lowest for r < 0). MODE: Normal (loop), Reverse, Pendulum, Random (every TIME a jump to a random sixteenth), Rand Start (each loop starts at a random sixteenth), Rand End (plays a random whole number of TIME intervals, then returns to 0), One Shot (once, then stops), Static (at every (re)trigger the events at position 0 sound and the playhead does not move). STEP MODE: Normal; New Only (an event plays only notes that arrived since the previous event); Chord (every event plays all of H, voiced by row: inversion `r−1` raises the lowest `r−1` notes an octave); Chord (new) (chord of the new notes only). Accent: velocity × 1.3 (≤ 127). Strum (amount a at t): chord notes start `j·a·D/N` later in ascending order (D = step duration). Lanes (129-point tables over the pattern): `vel` replaces the velocity (y·127), `gate` × gate, `chance` × chance, `x` → expression X of the generated notes.

#### 7.4.4 Timing, gate, chance
Step k starts at `t0 + k·D` (swung), `D` = ladder beats (BPM) or `1/(0.5·100^n)` s (Hz). Note-off at `+ gate·D` (gate value 0–2; > 1 overlaps; a repeated pitch retriggers). CHANCE c: each step sounds with probability c (sequencer PRNG); default the sequence advances anyway, **Pre**: a silent step does not advance.

#### 7.4.5 Latch, THRU, retrigger, velocity
- LATCH: released keys stay held until a key is pressed while none is physically down (the set is then replaced). While `arp.on`, the sustain pedal (CC64) drives latch instead of sustaining voices (inv B22 "CC64 sustain controls latch"): pedal down → `cmd('latch', 1)` = latch on (whatever the slot's LATCH button); pedal up → `cmd('latch', 0)` = latch returns to the slot's LATCH button value; if that is off, the latched notes that are not physically held are released (the held set becomes the physically held keys).
- THRU: input notes are also sent on unchanged.
- RETRIGGER: LAUNCH (slot launch restarts), RATE (restart every `RRATE_LADDER` interval on the grid), NOTE (any incoming note-on restarts), FIRST (a note-on while H was empty restarts). Restart = shape position 0, pass 0, repeats counter 0, velocity decay reset when VELOCITY RETRIG.
- VELOCITY (enable): `v_run` starts at the input velocity; per step `v_run += (T − v_run)·0.5·|dec|`, `T = dec ≥ 0 ? target·127 : (1 − target)·127` (dec = eff `arp.vdecay` value). Off: the source note's velocity.

#### 7.4.6 Slots
Notes of the trigger octave (raw MIDI note `12·(trigOct−1) + j`, j = 0…11) select and launch slot j (quantised to LAUNCH QUANT while running) and are consumed; the UI play buttons and the ARP keyboard send the same command (§12.4). "Edit All" is a UI behaviour (edits write every slot).

### 7.5 Clip player (inv B23)

#### 7.5.1 Launch and stop
Launch sources: slot play button / CLIP keyboard (UI command), trigger-octave note (`clip index = n − 12·(trigOct−1)`), input notes when `kb ≠ Off` (launch the selected clip). MONO: a launch stops the playing clip at the same quantised time; POLY: clips play together. Stop: the slot's stop button, the same CLIP key again, NOTE GATE release, One Shot end, `clip.on` off.

#### 7.5.2 Quantised start and RETRIG
Start at the next multiple of the clip's LAUNCH QUANT on the timebase (Off: at once). RETRIG on: playhead = `off + eff clip.offset·len` (sixteenths, wrapped into [start, len)). RETRIG off: playhead = `start + mod(4·b − 4·b_anchor, len − start)` with `b_anchor` = 0 (song zero / free-clock zero): the clip plays as if it had run since the anchor.

#### 7.5.3 Playhead and modes
Advances `4·rate` sixteenths per beat (rate = `CLIP_RATES[eff clip.rate]`) over `[start, len)`. Modes: Normal (loop) · Reverse · Pendulum · One Shot · Random / Rand.No Dup (every TIME slice jump to a random slice start) · Rand.Start (each loop starts at a random slice) · Rand.End (a random number of slices, then back to start) · Static (only the events at the start position are sent; frozen playhead).

#### 7.5.4 KB SPAN
Mono: the latest held key k transposes subsequent notes by `k − 60`; NOTE GATE: releasing all keys stops the clip. Poly: every held key runs its own playhead copy transposed by `k − 60` (≤ 8 copies, oldest stolen); NOTE GATE stops a copy at its key's release. Offset: key k starts the clip at `(k − 60)·offQ` beats (wrapped). Off: input notes do not affect the clip.

#### 7.5.5 Notes and lanes
Unmuted notes sound at their time with pitch `note + span transpose + eff clip.trans + kbd.trans` (then key/scale), velocity `vel` (× trigger velocity when VELO TRIG); a note crossing the loop end is cut there. Note expression x/y/z goes with the note. Macro lanes `m1…m8`: while a clip with lane mk plays, macro k's base is the lane value at the playhead (`proc.seqMacro[k]`, NaN otherwise; POLY: the most recently launched clip wins).

#### 7.5.6 Clip editing actions with state effects (UI + seq-core; normative definitions)
- **Send to Selected Clip** (sample slicing): `slicesToNotes(marks, lenSec, bpm, root)` (§11.3) replaces the notes and length of `clip.clips[clip.sel]` (one undo step).
- **Auto-Sync to Clip** (`smpo.slice.sync = 1`): while set, every change of the oscillator's marks, threshold, slice root or slice mode reruns Send to Selected Clip automatically — each update one panel-undo step, rate-limited to one per 200 ms (the last change always lands). Turning it off leaves the clip as it is.
- **Wrap Clip to Begin Here** (offset-marker menu): `seq-core wrapAt(notes, start, off, len)` rotates the region `[start, len)` so that the offset marker becomes its start: a note at t ≥ off moves to `t − off + start`, a note at t < off to `t + (len − off)`; a note crossing the wrap point is split into two notes; then `off = start`.
- **Record EXTEND**: while recording past the clip end, `len` grows by whole bars (16 sixteenths at a time) up to 1024; OVERDUB loops instead.
- **Export MIDI File** (slot menu; inv B23 "drag clip to DAW", deviation 21): `seq-core clipToSmf(clip, bpm) → Uint8Array` — SMF type 0, 960 PPQ, one tempo meta event (`bpm`), time signature 4/4, note events at `tick = round(240·t16)`, length `max(1, round(240·len16))`, velocity `vel`, muted notes skipped, notes outside `[start, len)` skipped and times taken relative to `start`, end-of-track at `240·(len − start)`; downloaded as `<clip name or "Clip n">.mid`; desktop drag of the slot uses `DownloadURL` where supported.
- **Copy to Track ▸** (slot menu): `host.clipToTrack(trackId, notes)` writes the clip's unmuted notes (song.js units: `t = playhead + round(240·(t16 − start))` ticks, `d = max(1, round(240·len16))`, `n = note`, `v = vel`) into the target track's song data at the song playhead — one project-history step (main.js uses song.js `pasteNotes`).

### 7.6 Keyboard panel (inv B21)
Transpose −24…+24 (input and generated notes, once); KEY/SCALE quantise (§3.15.7) on input after transpose and on generated notes; SWING (§7.2); OSC MAPPING ranges are evaluated by the voice (§6.2); the ARP row limits which notes reach the arpeggiator.

### 7.7 Engine contract
